<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, April 14, 2020
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, April 14, 2020, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
As we work through this time of the COVID-19 virus event please see the following information regarding access to the
Navajo County Board of Supervisors' meetings.
 
Per the most recent guidelines from the federal government that no more than 10 people should be gathered in a room at the same
time, the public will not be allowed in the Board Chambers.
Members of the public may call and listen to the meeting by following these steps: 
USA/Canada (toll free): Dial 1-877-820-7831
Enter the Passcode 942688
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions: 
Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov 1.
Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.2.
Name & Telephone Number 3.
Comments or questions should be sent by April 13, 2020, by 5:00 pm. for the April 14, 2020, Board meeting. 4.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: March 23, 2020, Emergency and March 27, 2020, Regular  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 March 2020  
 
c. Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5, March and Pinetop-Lakeside, Precinct #6, March
2020
 
 
d. Re-appointment of Jon H. Saline, State Bar #028445, as a judge pro tempore to serve the
Superior Court in Navajo County for a term beginning July 1, 2020, and ending June 30, 2021
 
 
e. Gila River Indian Community Grant Application to replace 3, 12 year old, Zoll E-Series
Monitor/Defibrillators

<!-- page 2 -->
f. Approval of 10 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $6,617.96  
 
g. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: Amendment
Number 1 Professional Services Agreement with Paul Watson and Authorization of Services
with Ironside Engineering & Development for Traffic Statement Review
 
 
h. 2020 Fair Housing Proclamation, proclaiming April as "Fair Housing" month in Navajo County  
 
i. Mutual Aid Agreement between the Sheriff's Office and the Hopi Tribe for coordinated law
enforcement efforts on the Hopi Reservation
 
 
j. Application for Treasurer's Deed for the Board of Supervisors on behalf of the State of Arizona  
 
k. Proclamation declaring April 2020 as "Child Abuse Prevention" month  
 
l. Authorization to pay overtime compensation, following the declaration of emergency on March
23, 2020, to select exempt employees
 
 
m. Proclamation declaring April 12 through 18, 2020 as "National Telecommunicator's Week"  
 
3. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Renewal of Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County nutrition program and
Holbrook Education Day Care Center
 
 
2. Renewal of Memorandum of Agreement with nutrition program and Holbrook Senior Center  
 
3. Renewal of Memorandum of Agreement with nutrition program and the City of Holbrook  
 
4. Renewal of Memorandum of Agreement with nutrition program and Taylor Elementary  
 
5. Intergovernmental Agreement with the Arizona Department of Health Services Amendment
Number 8 to Contract Number CTR042784 Public Health Emergency Preparedness -
COVID-19
 
 
b. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Update on COVID-19  
Presented By: Jeff Lee

<!-- page 3 -->
4. RECORDER:  
 
a. Consideration of the resignation of Doris Clark, Navajo County Recorder effective Friday, April
17, 2020, and appointing Michael Sample to fill the remainder of the Recorder's term.
 
Presented By: Doris Clark
 
5. TREASURER:  
 
a. Consideration of award of Contract B20-01-030 Bank Servicing Agreement to Wells Fargo
Bank, N.A.
 
Presented By: Kari Lopez
 
6. COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
 
a. EXECUTIVE SESSION pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for the Board to receive legal
advice from its attorney regarding the consideration and possible approval of extending a
settlement offer to resolve the valuation in tax years 2018 and 2019 in the Transwestern v.
ADOR litigation.
 
Presented By: Jason Moore
 
b. RECONVENE: Consideration and possible approval of extending a settlement offer to resolve
the valuation in tax years 2018 and 2019 in the Transwestern v. ADOR litigation.
 
 
7. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
8. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: April 10, 2020 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 10:00 a.m. By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. d.        
Meeting Date: 04/14/2020  
Title: Appointment of Judge pro tempore to the Superior Court in Navajo County 
Submitted For: Robert Higgins, Presiding Judge 
Submitted By: Lu Ann Garbini, Interim Court Administrator 
Department: Superior Court
Motion before the Board:
Re-appointment of Jon H. Saline, State Bar #028445, as a judge pro tempore to serve the Superior

Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
Narrative 
Please structure your proposal to provide the following information in the order indicated.  Provide the narrative in 
paragraph form in the text field provided.  Please be thorough but strive for brevity. 
1. Briefly describe the organization’s history, mission and goals.
THE HEBER-OVERGAARD FIRE DISTRICT PROVIDES FIRE, EMS AND AMBULANCE TRANSPORT SERVICE TO 
RESIDENTS AND VISITIORS TO THE COMMUNITIES OF HEBER-OVERGAARD LOCATED IN THE 
UNINCORPORATED AREA OF NAVAJO COUNTY. THE DISTRICT AMBULANCE SERVICE SERVES APPROXIMATELY 
3500 PERMANENT RESIDENTS OVER 200 SQUARE MILES AND A RECREATIONAL/SECOND HOME POPULATION 
THAT SWELLS TO 15000 IN THE SUMMER MONTHS. 55 MILES ARE STATE HIGHWAY 
2. If the applying organization is non-municipal or a non-profit 501c, please describe your relationship with Arizona
municipalities including past and present support, whether through partnerships, collaborations, in-kind
contributions, grants, tax-based funding or other.  Describe the supporting municipality’s role in this project.  If
the applying organization is a municipality governmental sub-division please enter the text “Not applicable.”
THE HEBER-OVERGAARD FIRE DISTRICT SERVES THOSE LOCATED IN AND SURROUNDING THE COMMUNITIES 
OF HEBER-OVERGAARD IN THE SOUTH-WESTERN PART OF NAVAJO COUNTY. LOCATED BETWEEN THE CITY 
OF SHOW LOW AND THE TOWN OF PAYSON. NAVAJO COUNTY COLLECTS PROPERTY TAX REVENUES FOR THE 
FIRE DISTRICT AND MAINTAINS THE MAIN BANK ACCOUNT FOR THE DISTRICT. THE FIRE DISTRICT WORKS 
CLOSELY WITH NAVAJO COUNTY ON ALL PUBLIC SAFETY AND PUBLIC EDUCATION PROJECTS. THE FIRE 
DISTRICT IS A KEY PLAYER IN THE WHITE MOUNTAIN AUTOAID CONSORTIUM WORKING CLOSELY WITH ALL 
PUBLIC SAFETY PARTNERS ON KEY COMMUNITY AND REGIONAL ISSUES.  
3. Describe the proposed project and your plan to implement.
THE HEBER-OVERGAARD FIRE DISTRICT OPERATES FIVE (5) ADVANCED LIFE SUPPORT (ALS) RESPONSE 
VEHICLES AND AN ALS FREQUENTED FIRE STATION TRIAGE ROOM. WE HAVE REMAINING IN USE, THREE (3) 
12 YEAR-OLD ZOLL E-SERIES MONITOR/DEFIBRILLATORS THAT ARE STILL IN SERVICE,  AT THEIR END OF 
SERVICE LIFE STAGE, AND NEED REPLACED. IF FUNDED THE EQUIPMENT COULD BE IMMEDIATELY ORDERED 
AND UPON DELIVERY PLACED INTO IMMEDIATE SERVICE TO THE COMMUNITY.

<!-- page 400 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
4. Describe how the proposed project satisfies one or more of the priority funding areas identified by the Gila River
Indian Community.
THE HEBER-OVERGAARD FIRE DISTRICT PROVIDES ADVANCED LIFE SUPPORT SERVICES TO A 200 SQUARE 
MILE AREA, INCLUDING 55 MILES OF STATE HIGHWAY. ADVANCED LIFE SUPPORT SERVICES IS A KEY 
COMPONENT OF PUBLIC SAFETY SERVICE. 
5. Identify the needs/problems to be addressed, target population and number of people to be served by the
project.
IN ADDITION TO THE DISTRICT’S RESPONSE AREA, OUR 3500 PRIMARY RESIDENTS ARE MOSTLY OF 
RETIREMENT AGE AND THE ELDERLY WITH A WIDE VARIETY OF CHRONIC AND ACUTE HEART AND 
CIRCULATORY SYSTEM AILMENTS. THE REPLACEMENT OF OUR OLD AND LIMITED CAPABILITY ZOLL E-SERIES 
MONITOR/DEFIBRILLATORS WITH THE ADVANCED X-SERIES DEVICES WOULD IMMEDIATELY IMPROVE THE 
DIRECT CARE PROVIDED TO A GREATER MAJORITY OF OUR RESIDENTS AND BRING MORE CAPABILITIES TO 
THE HANDS OF OUR PROVIDERS. 
6. Define the project as a new or continuing program.  Has GRIC previously funded this project?
THE REPLACEMENT PROJECT WOULD FURTHER CONTINUE OUR ADVANCED LIFE SUPPORT PROGRAM.  GRIC
ASSISTED HOFD IN REPLACING AN AGING AMBULANCE IN 2017 ALSO IN SUPPORT OF THIS PROGRAM.
7. Provide a brief timeline including start and finish dates.  Indicate if the timeline is flexible.
UPON APPROVAL OF THE GRANT, THE FIRE DISTRICT WILL IMMEDIATELY EXECUTE THE ZOLL ORDER FOR 
THREE (3) X-SERIES MONITOR/DEFIBRILLATORS MATCHING THE CAPABILITIES OF THE ONE RECENTLY 
REPLACED BY THE DISTRICT. UPON ORDER THE THREE (3) ZOLL X-SERIES WILL BE PLACED INSERVICE WITHIN 
6 MONTHS OF RECEIVING THE GRANT. 
8. Identify other organizations, partners or funders participating in the project and their roles.
NAVAJO COUNTY WILL BE THE FISCAL AGENT FOR THIS GRANT. AS AN ARIZONA FIRE DISTRICT UNDER ARE 
TITLE 48 THE COUNTY WILL MANAGE THE FUNDS. NAVAJO COUNTY WOULD SPONSOR THE HEBER-
OVERGAARD FIRE DISTRICT IN THE GRANT PROCESS, RECEIVE THE FUNDS AND UPON INVOICE FROM THE

<!-- page 401 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
VENDOR ISSUE A WARRANT FOR PAYMENT FOR THE EQUIPMENT. THE FIRE DISTRICT WOULD MANAGE THE 
GRANT, EXECUTE THE PURCHASE AND COMPLETE ALL NECESSARY GRANT REPORTS. 
9. Would you be able to implement the proposed plan if your organization received partial funding for this project?
YES 
10. Describe your plan for project financial sustainability beyond the grant period.  If this is a program/project
previously funded by the Gila River Indian Community describe efforts made towards the previously described
sustainability plan.
THE EQUIPMENT WILL BE OPERATED AND FULLY MAINTAINED UNDER MANUFACTURER SPECS FOR THE 
SERVICE LIFE OF THE EQUIPMENT BY THE FIRE DISTRICT. THE FIRE DISTRICT WILL ALSO COVER ALL SUPPLIES 
REQUIRED FOR ITS OPERATION. 
11. Describe your plan to document progress and results.
THE FIRE DISTRICT WILL MAINTAIN A GRANT FILE SPECIFIC FOR THIS PROJECT AND COMPLETE ALL 
NECESSARY GRANT REPORTING REQUIRMENTS MADE BY THE GILA RIVER INDIAN COMMUNITY. 
12. Indicate any application to and/or awards made by a tribe other than the Gila River Indian Community for state
shared revenues for this and any other project for the past five (5) years.  If this information is included in a
separate attachment, please indicate that here.
NONE

GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS 
 
1. All required services outside SCOPE OF WORK outline will be provided upon the CLIENT'S request and will be billed at the rates quoted on the CURRENT FEE 
SCHEDULE. 
2. Fees outlined in this contract will be adhered to subject to site conditions and criteria set forth by the CLIENT and the requirements of all applicable governmental 
agencies, utility companies, etc., in effect on the date of the ENGINEER'S signing of this contract. When an architectural site plan, building plan or other plan is 
to be used as the basis for the ENGINEER'S plans or designs, no work by the ENGINEER will commence until fully dimensioned and client-approved plans 
have been received. Subsequent changes to the plans which require additional work by the ENGINEER will result in extra charges at the rates quoted on the 
CURRENT FEE SCHEDULE. 
3. And field staking outlined in the SCOPE OF WORK will be performed one time only for the fees quoted. Any restaking due to loss of stakes beyond the 
ENGINEER'S control will be billed at the rate quoted on the CURRENT FEE SCHEDULE. In addition, fees outlined for field survey and construction staking are 
subject to the ENGINEER being able to perform each item without delays which may occur beyond his control, such as hindrances due to the CLIENT'S 
equipment and/or employees operating in the ENGINEER'S survey line. 
4. In the event that a question or claim may arise as to an error or omission in the ENGINEER'S work or plans, the ENGINEER will assume no liability for errors or 
omissions unless notified within 48 hours of the CLIENT'S discovery of such. If notified within 48 hours, the ENGINEER will have the right to remedy any such 
errors or omissions within a reasonable and agreed upon time thereafter, at no additional cost to the CLIENT.  The ENGINEER will assume no liability for 
construction staking unless all stakes are maintained intact and verified as to their origin. 
5. The CLIENT shall request construction staking items a minimum of three (3) working days in advance of when desired. 
6. The CLIENT shall give authorization to the ENGINEER to commence each item of work as outlined in the SCOPE OF WORK. The CLIENT will be billed only 
for work performed. 
7. CLIENT will be billed monthly, based upon percentage of work completed and/or hourly charges and reimbursable costs. A finance charge of one and one half 
percent (1½%) per month (18% annual percentage rate) will be added to portions of accounts over 45 days past due and will result in immediate stoppage of 
all services until payment is received or other written agreements made. Any and all charges incurred by the ENGINEER to collect past due accounts will be 
paid by the CLIENT. In the event litigation is instituted to enforce the provisions hereof the prevailing party or parties shall be entitled to recover from the other 
party or parties in addition to all other relief to which such prevailing party or parties may otherwise be entitled all costs, expenses and fees incurred by such 
party or parties (including such attorney's fees as shall be fixed by the court sitting without a jury.) 
8. The CLIENT hereby agrees that the balances as stated on the billing from the ENGINEER is correct, conclusive and binding on the CLIENT unless the CLIENT  
notifies the ENGINEER in writing within ten (10) days of the billing date of the particular item that is alleged to be incorrect. 
9. The obligation to provide further services under the SCOPE OF WORK may be terminated by either party upon seven (7) day's written notice. In the event of 
any termination, the ENGINEER will be paid for all services rendered to the date of termination plus unpaid reimbursable expenses. 
10. The ENGINEER will not be responsible for the cost of permits, title company charges, governmental review fees, soils reports, printing photographic charges, 
etc., as applicable, except those printing charges necessary for the ENGINEER to do his work. The ENGINEER will be reimbursed for such charges paid by 
him for the CLIENT at the rates quoted on the CURRENT FEE SCHEDULE. 
11. The ENGINEER will not be responsible or liable for the following: 
a) Any use of plans, surveys, specifications, etc., not signed and sealed by the ENGINEER and approved by the appropriate governmental agencies. 
b) Inaccuracy of data, plans, legal descriptions or any other information supplied by the CLIENT. 
c) Site soil and geologic conditions. 
d) Changes to the plans and specifications made by the CLIENT or others. 
e) Job site conditions. 
f) The performance of work on this project by any construction contractor or third party. 
12. All original work will be property of the ENGINEER. The CLIENT, at his request, will by furnished with reproducible copies of any and all work. 
13. All documents furnished by the ENGINEER are instruments of his service. They are not intended to be suitable for reuse or extensions of this project or any 
other project. Any reuse without specific written approval by the ENGINEER will be at the sole risk of the user and without liability or legal exposure to the 
ENGINEER.  
14. Neither the CLIENT nor ENGINEER shall assign, sublet or transfer any rights under or interest in this contract without the written consent of the other. Nothing 
herein shall be construed to give any rights or benefits hereunder to anyone other than the CLIENT or ENGINEER. 
15. The ENGINEER makes no representation concerning any cost estimate figures made in connection with maps, plans, specifications or drawings other than that 
all cost figures are estimates only and the ENGINEER shall not be responsible for fluctuations in costs or quantity figures. 
16. The CLIENT agrees to cooperate in every way requested by the ENGINEER to expedite the completion of the work set forth in the contract. The CLIENT agrees 
to provide the ENGINEER access to the property involved and to make available any records, documents, deeds, legal descriptions or other items requested 
by the ENGINEER for the reasonable pursuit of the completion of the work. 
17. The ENGINEER makes no warranty either expressed or implied, as to his findings, recommendations, specifications or professional advice except that these 
were promulgated after being prepared in accordance with generally accepted engineering and/or surveying practices and under the direction of registered 
professional engineers and/or licensed land surveyors. 
 
18. The preparation of Environmental Impact Statements and/or similar environmental  
 documents is not included in this contract.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. h.        
Meeting Date: 04/14/2020  
Title: 2020 Fair Housing Resolution
Submitted For: Paige Peterson, Finance Director  Submitted By: Jackie
Walker,
Accountant
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Motion before the Board:
2020 Fair Housing Proclamation, proclaiming April as "Fair Housing" month in Navajo County
Background:
The Civil Rights Act of 1968 and Fair Housing Amendments Act of 1988 declare a national
policy prohibiting discrimination in the sale, rental, leasing and financing of housing or land to be
used in the construction of housing or in the provision of brokerage services on the basis of race,
color, religion, sex, disability, familial status, or national origin.  The Housing and Community
Development Act of 1974 as amended requires that all applicants for Community Development
Block Grant funds certify that they shall affirmatively further fair housing.  Navajo County's
proclamation and resolution declaring April 2020 Fair Housing Month for Navajo County and the
dissemination thereof will reaffirm the County's commitment to furthering fair housing.

Proclamation

<!-- page 413 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
Navajo County Proclamation 
 
 
FAIR HOUSING 
 
WHEREAS, The National Fair  Housing Law of 1986, as amended by the Fair Housing 
Amendments Act of 1988 prohibits discrimination in housing and declares it a national policy to 
provide within constitutional limits, for fair housing in the United States; and  
 
WHEREAS, the principle of Fair Housing is not only national law and national policy but a 
fundamental human concept and entitlement for all Americans; and  
 
WHEREAS, April has traditionally been designated as Fai r Housing Month in the United States;  
 
NOW, THEREFORE,  I, Jason E. Whiting, Chair of the Board of Supervisors, do proclaim April as 
Fair Housing Month in Navajo County and do hereby urge all citizens of this community to comply 
with the letter and spirit o f the Fair Housing Law.  
 
 
 
___________________________  
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman

Title: Mutual Aid Agmt between Sheriff's Office and the Hopi Tribe

Motion before the Board:
Mutual Aid Agreement between the Sheriff's Office and the Hopi Tribe for coordinated law
enforcement efforts on the Hopi Reservation
Background:
This agreement between the Hopi Tribe on behalf of the Hopi Law Enforcement Services (HLES)
and the Sheriff's Office (NCSO) is to provide for the enforcement of criminal and traffic laws of
the Tribe and the State of Arizona within Indian Country.  The Tribe, through Cross Commission,
will grant the powers necessary to NCSO deputies providing the authority necessary to enforce
these laws.
HLES will conduct a course for NCSO deputies on Hopi culture, jurisdiction and tribal laws
within thirty days of the execution of this agreement and prior to the deputy receiving cross
commission.
This agreement is effective upon the date of the last signature.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
NCSO Contract No.____________________ 
 
 
MUTUAL AID AGREEMENT  
BETWEEN 
THE HOPI TRIBE  
AND  
THE NAVAJO COUNTY SHERIFF’S OFFICE 
 
 
I. Parties 
This mutual aid agreement (“Agreement”) is entered into by and between the Hopi Tribe 
(the “Tribe”) on behalf of the Hopi Law Enforcement Services (“HLES”), and the Navajo 
County Sheriff’s Office (“NCSO”).  The HLES and NCSO may be referenced herein 
individually as a “Party” and collectively as the “Parties.” 
 
II. Purpose 
This Agreement is entered into to provide for the orderly and effective enforcement of the 
criminal and traffic laws of the Tribe and the State of Arizona within “Indian Country” (as 
defined in 18 U.S.C. § 1151) subject to the Tribe’s jurisdiction; to prev ent any jurisdictio n 
from becoming a sanctuary for violators of the laws of another jurisdiction; to prevent inter-
jurisdictional flight; and to foster greater respect for the laws of each jurisdiction by the 
more certain application thereof. This Agreemen t is based on mutual respect for and 
recognition of the inherent sovereignty of the Tribe and the State of Arizona and the laws 
enacted by each sovereign. 
 
III. Authorities 
The Hopi Tribal Council is the governing body of the Tribe and is authorized by Article 
VI, Section 1 of the Constitution and Bylaws of the Tribe (December 19, 1936) to negotiate 
and make contracts with the Federal, State and local governments. NCSO is authorized to 
enter into this Agreement pursuant to A.R.S. § 11-952 and § 13-3872. 
 
IV. No Creation of Third Party Rights; Use of Agreement as Evidence 
The Agreement does not create any substantive or procedural rights or benefits, civil or 
criminal, in favor of any person or entity not a Party, nor does it create a duty to respond 
not otherwise imposed by law.  No part of this Agreement may be used as evidence in any 
court proceeding by any Party or any successor, assignee, or subrogee of any Party unless 
the entire Agreement is also received into evidence.   
 
V. Territorial Application 
The geographical area covered by this Agreement is co -extensive with the boundaries of 
the Tribe’s jurisdiction and the State of Arizona.  Each Party acting under the authority of 
this Agreement may engage in fresh pursuit as allowed by applicable Tribal, Arizona 
and/or federal law. Nothing herein shall affect the authority of NCSO to pursue non-Indians 
on the reservation.

<!-- page 416 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
VI. Scope of Powers 
A. The Tribe grants NCSO Deputies those powers necessary to enforce the criminal 
and traffic laws of the Tribe, including the powers to conduct searches or make 
arrests for any violations thereof to the full extent as allowed by applicable law, 
hereinafter referred to as “mutual aid law enforcement certification.” 
 
B. The Parties recognize and acknowledge that HLES officers are certified through 
Arizona Peace Officer Standards and Training and thus pursuant to A.R.S. §13 -
3874(A) independently possess and exercise all law enforcement powers of peace 
officers in the State of Arizona. 
 
VII. Application for Certification 
A. The NCSO Sheriff or designee shall complete and submit the HLES’s Peace 
Officer Commission Card form (Exhibit 1) for each NCSO Deputy who is 
qualified for a mutual aid law enforcement certification.  An NCSO Deputy is 
qualified if he or she completes the required training pursuant to  Subsection C, 
satisfactorily meets all requirements on the Peace Officer Commission Card 
form, and the NCSO Sheriff or designee submits a statement of qualificatio ns 
that includes a law enforcement certification card for each applicant. 
 
B. The Tribe will submit an authorizing tribal resolution to the United States 
Department of Interior, Bureau of Indian Affairs (“BIA”) approving use of the 
NCSO Deputies Special Law Enforcement Commission (“SLEC”) within the 
Tribe’s jurisdiction.  The issuan ce of the SLEC is subject to meeting the 
requirements pursuant to Title 25, United States Code, Section 2804 (Pub. L. 
101-379), 25 C.F.R. Part 12, and the Tribal Law and Order Act (Pub. L. 111 -
211).   
 
C. Within thirty (30) days of the execution of this Agreement, the HLES agrees to 
conduct a course for NCSO Deputies on Hopi culture, jurisdiction and tribal laws 
in HLES’s territorial jurisdiction prior to receiving a mutual aid law enforcement 
certification by the HLES. 
 
D. Without undue delay, the HLES shall certify each applicant upon determining 
that the applicant is qualified for mutual aid law enforcement certification.  No 
applicant shall be denied a mutual aid law enforcement certification on the basis 
of race, creed, sex, or color. 
 
E. An NCSO Deputy shall remain commissioned under this Agreement during the 
term of the Agreement and so long as he or she continues his or her employment 
as an NCSO Deputy. NCSO shall timely notify the HLES of any commissio ned 
officer’s termination of employment with NCSO and return the HLES mutual 
law enforcement certification card within ten (10) calendar days of the date of 
termination. NCSO will timely notify the HLES if an NCSO Deputy who holds 
an HLES mutual aid certification is has been charged with or convicted of a 
criminal offense.

<!-- page 417 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
F. The HLES may, at any time, suspend or revoke any mutual aid law enforcement 
certification issued pursuant to this Agreement for reasons solely within the 
HLES’s discretion.  The HLES shall promptly notify NCSO in writing if HLES 
suspends or revokes the mutual aid certification of any NCSO Deputy. NCSO 
shall return the officer’s HLES mutual aid law enforcement certification card 
within ten (10) calendar days of receiving written notification. 
 
G. Each Party shall be responsible for the provision and maintenance of its own 
equipment, materials and supplies except in emergency situations w here it 
appears to the officers involved that sharing or use of equipment, materials or 
supplies is necessary. 
 
H. Nothing in this Agreement shall be construed as an employment contract of 
individual officers of the HLES or NCSO. 
 
VIII. Arrest and Custody Procedure 
A. Indian suspects arrested within the HLES’s territorial jurisdiction by any 
certified NCSO Deputy shall immediately be taken to the appropriate and nearest 
HLES detention facility for booking. 
 
B. A certified NCSO Deputy who arrests any Indian within the HLES’s territorial 
jurisdiction under any provision of the HLES’s criminal laws shall inform the 
arrestee of his or her rights relating to the Tribe’s criminal code. 
 
C. A certified NCSO Deputy who holds a SLEC issued by the BIA to enforce 
federal law and who arrests any Indian within the HLES’s territorial jurisdictio n 
for any offense under 18 U.S.C. § 1152 or 1153, or any other federal criminal 
law, shall inform the arrestee of his or her rights as required by federal law. Any 
exercise of the federal authority by a certified NCSO Deputy on the Hopi 
Reservation pursuant to the SLEC must be reported to the HLES Chief of Police 
or his or her designee as soon as practicable following such exercise of authority.  
If the arresting NCSO Deputy is not commissioned by the BIA, he or she shall 
hold the suspect until a federally commissioned officer may inform the suspect 
of his or her rights as required by federal law. 
 
D. In the event a federally commissioned HLES or NCSO Deputy makes an arrest 
under 18 U.S.C. § 1152 or 1153, or for any federal felony within the HLES’s 
territorial jurisdiction, the officer shall immediately notify an HLES criminal 
investigator or FBI agent so that the investigator or agent can proceed with the 
investigation and prosecution of the offender. 
 
E. A certified NCSO Deputy who arrests any Indian within the HLES’s territorial 
jurisdiction shall notify the HLES’s police dispatcher, via police radio, of the 
arrest, obtain a report number, prepare and submit a complete and accurate 
HLES arrest/booking report, submit the arrest report to the proper HLES police

<!-- page 418 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
district immediately for processing, forward the information and documents to 
the Office of the Hopi Tribal Prosecutor, and honor any Tribal court subpoena 
and summons relating to the arrest.  
 
IX. Investigations 
All investigations, including searches and seizures, shall be conducted pursuant to 
applicable Tribal, Arizona, and/or federal law. 
 
X. Forfeitures 
If any tangible items of contraband, including money, are seized fr om an Indian or non -
Indian pursuant to the criminal laws of the Tribe or the State of Arizona as a result of any 
investigation within the HLES’s territorial jurisdiction in which the HLES and NCSO 
participate together, the HLES and NCSO shall share in the distribution of any and all 
forfeited monies and items not otherwise legally destroyed.  Shares and proceeds from the 
sale of any and all items, including monies, will be distributed based on applicable Arizona 
and federal law and the relative contribution s of the participating agencies.  Relative 
contributions will be cooperatively evaluated and determined by participating agency 
heads or their authorized delegates. 
 
XI. Crime Statistics 
The Parties agree that crime statistics arising from arrests and investig ations shall be 
accounted for and maintained by the agency in whose jurisdiction the offense was 
committed. 
 
XII. Extradition 
Any Indian located within the HLES’s territorial jurisdiction who has violated Arizona 
criminal law and who seeks asylum from prosecution by the State of Arizona shall be 
extradited pursuant the Tribe Criminal Code, except if the arrest is as a result of fresh 
pursuit pursuant to Section V of this Agreement. 
 
XIII. Citations for Traffic Offenses 
A. A certified NCSO Deputy who stops any Indian for a traffic offense within the 
HLES’s territorial jurisdiction may issue a written warning or a citation into the 
appropriate Tribal court or make an arrest where permitted by Tribal law. 
 
B. An HLES officer who stops a non-Indian for a traffic offense outside the HLES’s 
territorial jurisdiction but within the State of Arizona may issue a verbal or written 
warning or a State of Arizona traffic citation or make an arrest where permitted 
by Arizona law. 
 
XIV. Supervision and Control 
Each Party, when exercising authority granted pursuant to this Agreement, will retain 
supervision, control, and responsibility for their officers and employees. 
 
XV. Compensation and Benefits

<!-- page 419 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
All HLES officers remain employees of the HLES.  The HLES shall be liable for HLES 
officers’ salaries, overtime (if any), benefits, workers’ compensation, and civil liabilities.  
Each HLES officer shall be deemed to be performing regular duties for the HLES while 
performing public safety services (including without limitation working an oc currence, 
traveling to testify, or testifying) pursuant to this Agreement. All NCSO Deputies remain 
employees of the State of Arizona. The State of Arizona shall remain liable for all NCSO 
Deputies’ salaries, overtime (if any), benefits, workers’ compensation, and civil liabilities.  
Each certified NCSO Deputy shall be deemed to be performing regular duties for the State 
of Arizona while performing public safety services (including without limitation working 
an occurrence, traveling to testify, or testifying) pursuant to this Agreement. 
 
Each Party shall provide for payment of the costs and expenses of performance of its 
obligations undertaken pursuant to this Agreement and no taxable event shall arise from 
this Agreement.  No fees or costs shall be charged by either Party for any work performed 
under this Agreement. The chief law enforcement officer for each Party shall direct and 
require his/her officers to travel and testify, under subpoena, to the court with jurisdictio n 
over the occurrence. 
 
XVI. Dispute Resolution 
In the event of a dispute, claim or controversy (“dispute”) arising out of or related to this 
Agreement, the Parties agree to meet or discuss as promptly as possible to informally 
resolve the dispute in good faith. In the event the Parties are unabl e to reach informal 
resolution, either Party, or both, may notify the other in writing of intent to terminate the 
Agreement in accordance with Section XVIII. 
 
XVII. Indemnification 
Each Party shall be responsible and liable for damages caused by its employees ac ting 
within the scope of their employment. Each Party (as "Indemnitor") agrees to indemnify, 
defend, and hold harmless the other Party (as "Indemnitee") from and against any and all 
claims, losses, liability, costs, or expenses (including reasonable attorn ey's fees) 
(hereinafter collectively referred to as "Claims") arising out of bodily injury of any person 
(including death) or property damage, but only to the extent that such Claims which result 
in vicarious/derivative liability to the Indemnitee are caus ed by the act, omissio n, 
negligence, misconduct, or other fault of the Indemnitor, its officers, officials, agents, 
employees, or volunteers. 
 
XVIII. Duration, Modification and Termination of Agreement 
This Agreement is for a period of five (5) years from the effective unless modified or 
terminated as described below. This Agreement may be renewed for one term of five (5) 
years. The renewal must be completed and approved at least thirty (30) calendar days prior 
to the expiration of the preceding five -year term.  Amendments to the Agreement are 
subject to approval by the appropriate authorities and must be in writing and signed by all 
Parties. Either Party may terminate the Agreement upon at least thirty (30) days written 
notice sent via certified, return receipt U.S. mail. 
 
XIX. Conflict of Interest

<!-- page 420 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
This Agreement is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-511. 
 
XX. Arbitration 
The parties agree to resolve all disputes arising out of or relating to this Agreement through 
arbitration, after exhausting any applicable administrative review, to the extent required by 
A.R.S. § 12 -1518’ provided, this agreement to arbitrate shall not be enforceable if it is 
interpreted as or held to expressly or impliedly impair, limit, or waive the sovereign 
immunity of the Tribe or of the State of Arizona and its agencies, or as consent to be sued, 
or as submission to the jurisdiction of any court. 
 
XXI. Sovereign Immunity 
Nothing in this Agreement shall be interpreted, either expressly or impliedly, as 
constituting an impairment, limitation, or waiver of the sovereign immunity of the Tribe or 
of the State of Arizona and its agencies, or as consent to be sued, or as submission to the 
jurisdiction of any court. The Parties agree that nothing in this Agreement alters or conveys 
any judicial jurisdiction, including but not limited to the authority to issue warrants for 
arrest or search and seizure, or to issue service of process. 
 
XXII. Notices 
All notices and communications required or permitted under this Agreement shall be in 
writing and shall eithe r be delivered in person or sent by certified mail, return receipt 
requested, to the intended recipient at the addresses set forth below (or such other address 
as a Party may hereafter specify in writing): 
 
 HLES: Chief of Police 
  Hopi Law Enforcement Services  
  P.O. Box 123 
  Kykotsmovi, AZ 86039 
 
 NCSO: Sheriff 
  Navajo County Sheriff’s Office  
  P.O. Box 668 
  137 West Arizona 
  Holbrook, AZ 8 6025 
 
XXIII. Savings Clause 
If any provision of this Agreement is held invalid or unenforceable by any court of 
competent jurisdiction, all remaining provisions shall remain in full force and effect. 
 
XXIV. Entire Agreement 
This Agreement, and any exhibits or other attachments hereto, constitutes the entire 
agreement between the Parties relating to the subject matter.  
 
XXV. Effective Date

<!-- page 421 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
This Agreement shall become effective on the date the last signature of the appropriate 
authorities is affixed below. This Agreement may be executed in up to four counterparts, 
each of which is to be treated as the original.

Title: Application for Treasurer's Deed for the State of Arizona

Treasurer
Department: Treasurer
Motion before the Board:
Application for Treasurer's Deed for the Board of Supervisors on behalf of the State of Arizona
Background:
APPROVAL BY THE BOARD PER A.R.S. §42-18261, wherein it reads: If a lien is not
redeemed at any time beginning five years after the lien is assigned to this state, the county board
of supervisors, acting on behalf of this state, may apply for and receive a treasurer's deed to the
property under this article.
Title searches must be performed on each parcel and each owner must be served with a certified
notice. If redemption be not made by law before August 31, 2020, the parcels will then be deeded
to the State of Arizona and turned over to the Board of Supervisors for auction.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 19/20
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 32,800.00
Fiscal Impact:
Approximately $32,800.00 in the Treasurer's budget.

Title: Child Abuse Prevention Month April 2020
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Proclamation declaring April 2020 as "Child Abuse Prevention" month 
Background:
April is National Child Abuse Prevention Month. This month and throughout the year, the Navajo
County Family Advocacy Center (FAC) encourages all individuals and organizations to play a
role in making our White Mountain Communities a better place for children and families.

Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 04/08/2020 04:54 PM

<!-- page 430 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
  
Child Abuse Prevention Month – April 2020 
 
WHEREAS, in Federal fiscal year 2018, 4.3 million reports were made to child protective 
services; and 
 
WHEREAS, the hidden epidemic of child abuse crosses all boundaries of income, race, religion, 
and ethnicity and continues to be one of more serious health issues with over 90% of abuse 
perpetrated by people that children know, love, and trust; and 
  
WHEREAS, child abuse and neglect are a serious problem affecting every segment of our 
community, and finding solutions requires input and action from everyone; and  
 
WHEREAS, our children are our most valuable resources and will shape the future of Navajo 
County; and  
 
WHEREAS, child abuse can have long‐term psychological, emotional, and physical effects that 
have lasting consequences for victims of abuse; and  
 
WHEREAS, protective factors are conditions that reduce or eliminate risk and promote the 
social, emotional, and developmental well‐being of children; and  
 
WHEREAS, effective child abuse prevention activities succeed because of the partnerships 
created between child welfare professionals, education, health, community‐ and faith‐based 
organizations, businesses, law enforcement agencies, and families; and  
 
WHEREAS, communities must make every effort to promote programs and activities that create 
strong and thriving children and families; and  
 
WHEREAS, we acknowledge that we must work together as a community to increase awareness 
about child abuse and contribute to promote the social and emotional well‐being of children 
and families in a safe, stable, and nurturing environment; and  
 
WHEREAS, prevention remains the best defense for our children and families.

<!-- page 431 -->
NOW, THEREFORE, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, do hereby proclaim April 2020 as  
NATIONAL CHILD ABUSE PREVENTION MONTH 
 in Navajo County and urge all citizens to recognize this month by dedicating ourselves to the 
task of improving the quality of life for all children and families. 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on April 14, 2020. 
 
         ____________________________ 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. l.        
Meeting Date: 04/14/2020  
Title: overtime compensation to select Exempt employees
Submitted By: Eric Scott, HR Director
Department: Administrative Services Division: Human Resources
Motion before the Board:
Authorization to pay overtime compensation, following the declaration of emergency on March
23, 2020, to select exempt employees
Background:
Policy 2.13 (J) was put in place to allow special authorization to pay exempt employees,
who work extraordinarily extended workdays during exceptional circumstances, pay above
their 40 hours each week. Exempt employees are exempt from overtime laws under the
Fair Labor Standards Act (FLSA) and therefore do not get compensated for hours worked
over 40 hours in a week. Approval of this authorization will allow select employees to be
compensated at a straight hourly rate of pay for hours worked over 40 during this declared
emergency regarding COVID-19. Approval of this will only apply to hours worked over 40
that are in relation to work on COVID-19 and will last through the duration of the declared
emergency. Employees paid additional compensation under this approval will be
individually approved by the County Manager.

Navajo County     
Proclamation 
National Telecommunicators Week
WHEREAS, emergencies can occur at any time that require police, fire or emergency medical 
services; and 
WHEREAS, when an emergency occurs the prompt response of police officers, firefighters and 
paramedics is critical to the protection of life and preservation of property; and 
WHEREAS, the safety of our police officers and firefighters is dependent upon the quality and 
accuracy of information obtained from citizens who telephone the Navajo County Sheriff’s 
police-fire communications center; and 
WHEREAS, Public Safety Telecommunicators are the first and most critical contact our citizens
have with emergency services; and 
WHEREAS, Public Safety Telecommunicators are the single vital link for our police officers and
firefighters by monitoring their activities by radio, providing them information and ensuring their
safety; and 
WHEREAS, Public Safety Telecommunicators of the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office have
contributed substantially to the apprehension of criminals, suppression of fires and treatment of 
patients; and 
WHEREAS, each dispatcher has exhibited compassion, understanding, and professionalism
during the performance of their job in the past year.  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County and the 
Navajo County Sheriff that the week of April 12 through April 18, 2020, is designated “National
Telecommunicators Week” in Navajo County, Arizona, in honor of the men and women whose
diligence and professionalism keep our county and citizens safe. 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on April 14, 2020.

Jason Whiting, Chairman of the Board

CONTRACT NUMBER INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
SCOPE OF WORK CTR042784 
 
Page 2 of 5 
 
1. Background 
 
The Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) supports the Public Health Crisis Notice of Funding Opportunity 
from the Department of Health and Human Services (HHS), Center of Disease Control and Prevention (CDC).   
 
2. Objective 
 
The goal of this project is to work with the CDC in a crisis situation to rapidly mobilize, surge, and respond to public 
health emergencies occurring in Arizona.   Arizona has been pre-approved for rapid funding by the CDC for public 
health emergencies of large scale, complexity, or consequence that it would have an overwhelming impact upon and 
exceed the resources available within the state. 
 
2.1. Strategies associated with the response are: 
 
2.1.1. Strengthen incident management, 
 
2.1.2. Strengthen jurisdictional recovery, 
 
2.1.3. Strengthen biosurveillance, 
 
2.1.4. Strengthen information management, 
 
2.1.5. Strengthen countermeasures and mitigation, and 
 
2.1.6. Strengthen surge management. 
 
2.2. Outcomes associated with these strategies include: 
 
2.2.1. Earliest possible activation and management of emergency operations, 
 
2.2.2. Earliest possible identification and investigation of an incident/index case, 
 
2.2.3. Timely implementation of interventions and control measures, 
 
2.2.4. Timely communication of risk and essential elements of information by partners, and 
 
2.2.5. Timely coordination and support of response activities with healthcare and other partners. 
 
3. Tasks 
 
3.1. The Contractor shall: 
 
3.1.1. Comply with existing and/or future directives and guidance from the HHS, CDC Secretary regarding 
control of the spread of COVID-19, 
 
3.1.2. Consult and coordinate with HHS,  CDC to provide, commensurate with the condition of the 
individual, COVID -19 patient care regardless of the individual’s home jurisdiction and/or 
appropriate public health measures (e.g., social distancing, home isolation), and 
 
3.1.3. Assist the United States Government in the implementation and enforcement of federal orders 
related to quarantine and isolation. 
 
3.2. HHS, CDC has established allowable activities related to the capability domains described in the Public 
Health Crisis Response Notice Of Funding Opportunity. The domains include: 
 
3.2.1. Incident Management for Early Crisis Response, 
 
3.2.2. Jurisdictional Recovery,

<!-- page 455 -->
CONTRACT NUMBER INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
SCOPE OF WORK CTR042784 
 
Page 3 of 5 
 
3.2.3. Information Management, 
 
3.2.4. Countermeasures and Mitigation, 
 
3.2.5. Surge Management, and 
 
3.2.6. Biosurveillance. 
 
3.3. Surveillance, Laboratory Testing, and Reporting  
 
3.3.1. Contractor shall implement and scale- up laboratory testing and data collection to enable 
identification and tracking of COVID-19 cases in the community and is responsible for immediate 
implementation of real-time reporting to the HPP, CDC. Specifically, jurisdictions should focus on 
the following activities, in accordance with CDC guidelines: 
 
3.3.1.1. Conduct surveillance to identify cases, report case data in a timely manner, identify 
contacts, characterize disease transmission, and track  relevant epidemiologic 
characteristics including hospitalization and death; 
 
3.3.1.2. Conduct surveillance to monitor virologic and disease activity in the community and 
healthcare settings; 
 
3.3.1.3. Implement routine and enhanced surveillance to support the science base that informs 
public health interventions that mitigate the impact of COVID-19, including understanding 
of clinical characteristics; infection prevention and control practices; and other mitigation 
requirements; 
 
3.3.1.4. Establish or enhance core epidemiological ac tivities to support  response such as risk 
assessment, case classification, analysis, visualization and reporting; 
 
3.3.1.5. Conduct surveillance to monitor disruption in the community caused by COVID -19 and 
related mitigation activities (e.g. school closures and cancellation of mass gatherings) ; 
and 
 
3.3.1.6. Conduct surveillance to monitor disruption in healthcare systems caused by COVID -19 
(e.g. shortages of personal protective equipment). 
 
3.4. Community Intervention Implementation Plan 
 
3.4.1. Contractor shall  develop a brief COVID -19 community intervention implementation plan that 
describes how the state and local jurisdictions will achieve the response’s three (3) mitigation goals: 
 
3.4.1.1. Slow transmission of disease; 
 
3.4.1.2. Minimize morbidity and mortality; and 
 
3.4.1.3. Preserve healthcare, workforce, and infrastructure functions and minimize social and 
economic impacts.  
 
3.4.2. The plan shall address: 
 
3.4.2.1. Minimizing potential spread and reduce morbidity and mortality of COVID -19 in 
communities; 
 
3.4.2.2. Planning and adapt ing for disrupti on caused by community spread and implement 
interventions to prevent further spread; 
 
3.4.2.3. Ensuring healthcare system response is an integrated part of community interventions ; 
and

<!-- page 456 -->
CONTRACT NUMBER INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
SCOPE OF WORK CTR042784 
 
Page 4 of 5 
 
 
3.4.2.4. Ensuring integration of community mitigation interventions with health system 
preparedness and response plans and interventions. 
 
4. Deliverables and Delivery Schedule 
 
4.1. The budget and performance period is March 5, 2020 through March 15, 2021, which can be used to cover 
certain pre-award costs dating back to January 20, 2020.  
 
4.2. The contractor shall: 
 
4.2.1. Develop a brief COVID-19 community intervention implementation plan as referenced in this Scope 
of Work, Provision Three (3) Tasks, Section 3.4 within six (6) months from receipt of funds, 
 
4.2.2. Submit a budget within thirty (30) days from receipt of funds,  
 
4.2.3. Submit work plans within ninety (90) days from receipt of funds, and 
 
4.2.4. Submit monthly contractor expenditure reports with detailed information and receipts. 
 
5. Notices, Correspondence, Reports and Invoices

Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T., March 31, 2020, in strict accordance with the

Presented By: Jason Moore
Motion before the Board:
EXECUTIVE SESSION pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for the Board to receive legal
advice from its attorney regarding the consideration and possible approval of extending a
settlement offer to resolve the valuation in tax years 2018 and 2019 in the Transwestern v. ADOR
litigation.
Background:
See confidential memorandum from Attorney General's Office.
Recommendation: 
Navajo County Attorney's Office recommends approval.

The fiscal impact to the County is minimal.
